</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>hevc_freq</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Frequency of the High Efficiency Video Codec block in MHz; individual override of the <code>gpu_freq</code> setting. Raspberry Pi 4 only.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>sdram_freq</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Frequency of the SDRAM in MHz. SDRAM overclocking on Raspberry Pi 4 or newer is not supported.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">CPU/GPU core upper voltage limit. The value should be in the range [-16,8] which equates to the range [0.95V,1.55V] ([0.8,1.4V] on Raspberry Pi 1) with 0.025V steps. In other words, specifying -16 will give 0.95V (0.8V on Raspberry Pi 1) as the maximum CPU/GPU core voltage, and specifying 8 will allow up to 1.55V (1.4V on Raspberry Pi 1). For defaults, see the table below. Values above 6 are only allowed when <code>force_turbo=1</code> is specified: this sets the warranty bit if <code>over_voltage_*</code> &gt; <code>0</code> is also set.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Sets <code>over_voltage_sdram_c</code>, <code>over_voltage_sdram_i</code>, and <code>over_voltage_sdram_p</code> together.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram_c</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">SDRAM controller voltage adjustment. [-16,8] equates to [0.8V,1.4V] with 0.025V steps. Not supported on Raspberry Pi 4 or later devices.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram_i</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">SDRAM I/O voltage adjustment. [-16,8] equates to [0.8V,1.4V] with 0.025V steps. Not supported on Raspberry Pi 4 or later devices.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram_p</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">SDRAM phy voltage adjustment. [-16,8] equates to [0.8V,1.4V] with 0.025V steps. Not supported on Raspberry Pi 4 or later devices.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>force_turbo</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Forces turbo mode frequencies even when the Arm cores are not busy. Enabling this might set the warranty bit if <code>over_voltage_*</code> is also set.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>initial_turbo</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">
<p class="tableblock">Enables <a href="https://forums.raspberrypi.com/viewtopic.php?f=29&amp;t=6201&amp;start=425#p180099">turbo mode from boot</a> for the given value in seconds, or until <code>cpufreq</code> sets a frequency. The maximum value is <code>60</code>. The November 2024 firmware update made the following changes:</p>
<p class="tableblock">* changed the default from <code>0</code> to <code>60</code> to reduce boot time
* switched the kernel CPU performance governor from <code>powersave</code> to <code>ondemand</code></p>
</td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>arm_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>arm_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking. Note that reducing this value below the default does not result in any significant power savings, and is not currently supported.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>core_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>core_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>gpu_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>gpu_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>h264_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>h264_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>isp_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>isp_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>v3d_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>v3d_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>hevc_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>hevc_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>sdram_freq_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>sdram_freq</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Minimum value of <code>over_voltage</code> used for dynamic frequency clocking. The value should be in the range [-16,8] which equates to the range [0.8V,1.4V] with 0.025V steps. In other words, specifying -16 will give 0.8V as the CPU/GPU core idle voltage, and specifying 8 will give a minimum of 1.4V. This setting is deprecated on Raspberry Pi 4 and Raspberry Pi 5.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_delta</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">On Raspberry Pi 4 and Raspberry Pi 5 the over_voltage_delta parameter adds the given offset in microvolts to the number calculated by the DVFS algorithm.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>temp_limit</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Overheat protection. This sets the clocks and voltages to default when the SoC reaches this value in degree Celsius. Values over 85 are clamped to 85.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>temp_soft_limit</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><strong>3A+/3B+ only</strong>. CPU speed throttle control. This sets the temperature at which the CPU clock speed throttling system activates. At this temperature, the clock speed is reduced from 1400 MHz to 1200 MHz.  Defaults to <code>60</code>, can be raised to a maximum of <code>70</code>, but this might cause instability.</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>core_freq_fixed</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Setting to 1 disables active scaling of the core clock frequency and ensures that any peripherals that use the core clock will maintain a consistent speed. The fixed clock speed is the higher/turbo frequency for the platform in use. Use this in preference to setting specific core_clock frequencies as it provides portability of config files between platforms.</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="paragraph">
<p>This table gives the default values for the options on various Raspberry Pi models, all frequencies are stated in MHz.</p>
</div>

</tr></tbody></table><div class="paragraph">
<p>This table gives defaults for options which are the same across all models.</p>
</div>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><thead><tr><th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Option</th>
<th class="tableblock halign-center valign-top">Default</th>
</tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>initial_turbo</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (seconds)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>temp_limit</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">85 (°C)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (1.35V, 1.2V on Raspberry Pi 1)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_min</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (1.2V)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (1.2V)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram_c</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (1.2V)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram_i</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (1.2V)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>over_voltage_sdram_p</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-center valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0 (1.2V)</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="paragraph">
<p>The firmware uses Adaptive Voltage Scaling (AVS) to determine the optimum CPU/GPU core voltage in the range defined by <code>over_voltage</code> and <code>over_voltage_min</code>.</p>
</div>
<h4 id="specific-to-raspberry-pi-4-raspberry-pi-400-and-cm4" class="discrete">Specific to Raspberry Pi 4, Raspberry Pi 400 and CM4</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The minimum core frequency when the system is idle must be fast enough to support the highest pixel clock (ignoring blanking) of the display(s). Consequently, <code>core_freq</code> will be boosted above 500 MHz if the display mode is 4Kp60.</p>
</div>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><thead><tr><th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Display option</th>
<th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Max <code>core_freq</code>
</th>
</tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Default</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">500</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>hdmi_enable_4kp60</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">550</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
There is no need to use <code>hdmi_enable_4kp60</code> on Flagship models since Raspberry Pi 5, Compute Modules since CM5, and Keyboard models since Pi 500; they support dual-4Kp60 displays by default.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p>Overclocking requires the latest firmware release.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>The latest firmware automatically scales up the voltage if the system is overclocked. Manually setting <code>over_voltage</code> disables automatic voltage scaling for overclocking.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>It is recommended when overclocking to use the individual frequency settings (<code>isp_freq</code>, <code>v3d_freq</code> etc) rather than <code>gpu_freq</code>, because the maximum stable frequency will be different for ISP, V3D, HEVC etc.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>The SDRAM frequency is not configurable on Raspberry Pi 4 or later devices.</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="force_turbo">
<a class="anchor" href="#force_turbo"></a><code>force_turbo</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>By default (<code>force_turbo=0</code>) the on-demand CPU frequency driver will raise clocks to their maximum frequencies when the Arm cores are busy, and will lower them to the minimum frequencies when the Arm cores are idle.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>force_turbo=1</code> overrides this behaviour and forces maximum frequencies even when the Arm cores are not busy.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="clocks-relationship">
<a class="anchor" href="#clocks-relationship"></a>Clocks relationship</h3>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="raspberry-pi-4">
<a class="anchor" href="#raspberry-pi-4"></a>Raspberry Pi 4</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The GPU core, CPU, SDRAM and GPU each have their own PLLs and can have unrelated frequencies. The h264, v3d and ISP blocks share a PLL.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To view the Raspberry Pi device’s current frequency in KHz, type: <code>cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/cpufreq/scaling_cur_freq</code>. Divide the result by 1000 to find the value in MHz. Note that this frequency is the kernel <em>requested</em> frequency, and it is possible that any throttling (for example at high temperatures) might mean the CPU is actually running more slowly than reported. An instantaneous measurement of the actual Arm CPU frequency can be retrieved using the vcgencmd <code>vcgencmd measure_clock arm</code>. This is displayed in Hertz.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="monitoring-core-temperature">
<a class="anchor" href="#monitoring-core-temperature"></a>Monitoring core temperature</h3>
<div class="sidebarblock whitepaper"><div class="content">
<div class="title"><h5><a href="https://pip.raspberrypi.com/documents/RP-003608-WP" target="_blank">Cooling a Raspberry Pi device</a></h5></div>
<div class="inner">
<div class="coverimage" data-link="https://pip.raspberrypi.com/documents/RP-003608-WP">
<p class="title">Cooling a Raspberry Pi device</p>
<p class="subtitle"></p>
</div>
<div><div class="paragraph">
<p>This white paper goes through the reasons why your Raspberry Pi might get hot and why you might want to cool it back down, offering options on the cooling process.</p>
</div></div>
</div>
</div></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To view the temperature of a Raspberry Pi, run the following command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">cat</span> /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone0/temp</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Divide the result by 1000 to find the value in degrees Celsius. Alternatively, you can use <code>vcgencmd measure_temp</code> to report the GPU temperature.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Hitting the temperature limit is not harmful to the SoC, but it will cause the CPU to throttle. A heatsink can help to control the core temperature, and therefore performance. This is especially useful if the Raspberry Pi is running inside a case. Airflow over the heatsink makes cooling more efficient.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>When the core temperature is between 80°C and 85°C, the Arm cores will be throttled back. If the temperature exceeds 85°C, the Arm cores and the GPU will be throttled back.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>For the Raspberry Pi 3 Model B+, the PCB technology has been changed to provide better heat dissipation and increased thermal mass. In addition, a soft temperature limit has been introduced, with the goal of maximising the time for which a device can "sprint" before reaching the hard limit at 85°C. When the soft limit is reached, the clock speed is reduced from 1.4 GHz to 1.2 GHz, and the operating voltage is reduced slightly. This reduces the rate of temperature increase: we trade a short period at 1.4 GHz for a longer period at 1.2 GHz. By default, the soft limit is 60°C. This can be changed by the <code>temp_soft_limit</code> setting in <code>config.txt</code>.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="monitoring-voltage">
<a class="anchor" href="#monitoring-voltage"></a>Monitoring voltage</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>It is essential to keep the supply voltage above 4.8 V for reliable performance. Note that the voltage from some USB chargers/power supplies can fall as low as 4.2 V. This is because they are usually designed to charge a 3.7 V LiPo battery, not to supply 5 V to a computer.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To monitor the Raspberry Pi device’s PSU voltage, you will need to use a multimeter to measure between the VCC and GND pins on the GPIO. More information is available in the <a href="raspberry-pi.html#power-supply">power</a> section of the documentation.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If the voltage drops below 4.63 V (±5%), the Arm cores and the GPU are throttled back, and a message indicating the low voltage state is added to the kernel log.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The Raspberry Pi 5 PMIC has built in ADCs that allow the supply voltage to be measured. To view the current supply voltage, run the following command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>vcgencmd pmic_read_adc EXT5V_V</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="overclocking-problems">
<a class="anchor" href="#overclocking-problems"></a>Overclocking problems</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Most overclocking issues show up immediately, when the device fails to boot. If your device fails to boot due to an overclocking configuration change, use the following steps to return your device to a bootable state:</p>
</div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p>Remove any clock frequency overrides from <code>config.txt</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Increase the core voltage using <code>over_voltage_delta</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Re-apply overclocking parameters, taking care to avoid the previous known-bad overclocking parameters.</p>
</li>
</ol></div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="conditional-filters">
<a class="anchor" href="#conditional-filters"></a>Conditional filters</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/config_txt/conditional.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>When a single SD card (or card image) is being used with one Raspberry Pi and one monitor, it is easy to set <code>config.txt</code> as required for that specific combination and keep it that way, amending it only when something changes.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>However, if one Raspberry Pi is swapped between different monitors, or if the SD card (or card image) is being swapped between multiple boards, a single set of settings might no longer be sufficient. Conditional filters allow you to define certain sections of the config file to be used only in specific cases, allowing a single <code>config.txt</code> to create different configurations when read by different hardware.</p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-all-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-all-filter"></a>The <code>[all]</code> filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>[all]</code> filter is the most basic filter. It resets all previously set filters and allows any settings listed below it to be applied to all hardware. It is usually a good idea to add an <code>[all]</code> filter at the end of groups of filtered settings to avoid unintentionally combining filters (see below).</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="model-filters">
<a class="anchor" href="#model-filters"></a>Model filters</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The conditional model filters apply according to the following table.</p>
</div>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><thead><tr><th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Filter</th>
<th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Applicable models</th>
</tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi1]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">1A, 1B, 1A+, 1B+, Compute Module 1</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi2]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">2B (BCM2836- or BCM2837-based)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi3]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">3B, 3B+, 3A+, Compute Module 3, Compute Module 3+</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi3+]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">3A+, 3B+ (also sees <code>[pi3]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi4]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">4B, 400, Compute Module 4, Compute Module 4S</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi5]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">5, 500, 500+, Compute Module 5</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi400]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">400 (also sees <code>[pi4]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi500]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">500/500+ (also sees <code>[pi5]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm0]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 0 (also sees <code>[pi02]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm1]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 1 (also sees <code>[pi1]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm3]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 3 (also sees <code>[pi3]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm3+]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 3+ (also sees <code>[pi3+]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm4]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 4 (also sees <code>[pi4]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm4s]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 4S (also sees <code>[pi4]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[cm5]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Compute Module 5 (also sees <code>[pi5]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi0]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Zero, Zero W, Zero 2 W</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi0w]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Zero W (also sees <code>[pi0]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[pi02]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Zero 2 W (also sees <code>[pi0w]</code> and <code>[pi0]</code> contents)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>[board-type=Type]</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Filter by <code>Type</code> number – see <a href="raspberry-pi.html#raspberry-pi-revision-codes">Raspberry Pi Revision Codes</a>. For example,  <code>[board-type=0x14]</code> matches CM4.</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="paragraph">
<p>These are particularly useful for defining different <code>kernel</code>, <code>initramfs</code>, and <code>cmdline</code> settings, as the Raspberry Pi 1 and Raspberry Pi 2 require different kernels. They can also be useful to define different overclocking settings, as the Raspberry Pi 1 and Raspberry Pi 2 have different default speeds. For example, to define separate <code>initramfs</code> images for each:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre>[pi1]
initramfs initrd.img-3.18.7+ followkernel
[pi2]
initramfs initrd.img-3.18.7-v7+ followkernel
[all]</pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Remember to use the <code>[all]</code> filter at the end, so that any subsequent settings aren’t limited to Raspberry Pi 2 hardware only.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Some models of Raspberry Pi, including Zero, Compute Module, and Keyboard models, read settings from multiple filters. To apply a setting to only one model:</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p>apply the setting to the base model (for example, <code>[pi4]</code>), then revert the setting for all models that read the base model’s filters (for example, <code>[pi400]</code>, <code>[cm4]</code>, <code>[cm4s]</code>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>use the <code>board-type</code> filter with a revision code to target a single model (for example, <code>[board-type=0x11]</code>)</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
</td>
</tr></table></div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-none-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-none-filter"></a>The <code>[none]</code> filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>[none]</code> filter prevents any settings that follow from being applied to any hardware. Although there is nothing that you can’t do without <code>[none]</code>, it can be a useful way to keep groups of unused settings in config.txt without having to comment out every line.</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="c"># Bootloader EEPROM config.
# If PM_RSTS is partition 62 then set bootloader properties to disable
# SD high speed and show HDMI diagnostics
# Boot from partition 2 with debug option.
</span><span class="nn">[partition=62]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># Only high (&gt;31) partition can be remapped.
</span><span class="py">PARTITION</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">2</span>
<span class="py">SD_QUIRKS</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">0x1</span>
<span class="py">HDMI_DELAY</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">0</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Example <code>config.txt</code> – (currently, Raspberry Pi 5 onwards)</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="c"># config.txt – If the original requested partition number in PM_RSTS was a
# special number then use an alternate cmdline.txt
</span><span class="nn">[partition=62]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="py">cmdline</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">cmdline-recovery.txt</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The raw value of the <code>PM_RSTS</code> register at bootup is available through <code>/proc/device-tree/chosen/bootloader/rsts</code> and the final partition number used for booting is available through <code>/proc/device-tree/chosen/bootloader/partition</code>. These are big-endian binary values.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-expression-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-expression-filter"></a>The expression filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The expression filter provides support for comparing unsigned integer "boot variables" to constants using a simple set of operators. It is intended to support OTA update mechanisms, debug and test.</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p>The "boot variables" are <code>boot_arg1</code>, <code>cust_otpN</code>, <code>bootvar0</code>, <code>boot_count</code>, <code>boot_partition</code> and <code>partition</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Boot variables are always lower case.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Integer constants can either be written as decimal or as hex.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Expression conditional filters have no side-effects, for example, no assignment operators.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>As with other filter types the expression filter cannot be nested.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Use the <code>[all]</code> filter to reset expressions and all other conditional filter types.</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Syntax:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="c"># ARG is a boot-variable
# VALUE and MASK are unsigned integer constants
</span><span class="nn">[ARG=VALUE]</span><span class="w">      </span><span class="c"># selected if (ARG == VALUE)
</span><span class="nn">[ARG&amp;MASK]</span><span class="w">       </span><span class="c"># selected if ((ARG &amp; VALUE) != 0)
</span><span class="nn">[ARG&amp;MASK=VALUE]</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="c"># selected if ((ARG &amp; MASK) == VALUE)
</span><span class="nn">[ARG&lt;VALUE]</span><span class="w">      </span><span class="c"># selected if (ARG &lt; VALUE)
</span><span class="nn">[ARG&gt;VALUE]</span><span class="w">      </span><span class="c"># selected if (ARG &gt; VALUE)
</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="boot_arg1">
<a class="anchor" href="#boot_arg1"></a><code>boot_arg1</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi 5 and newer devices only.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>boot_arg1</code> variable is a 32-bit user defined value which is stored in a reset-safe register allowing parameters to be passed across a reboot.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Setting <code>boot_arg1</code> to 42 in <code>config.txt</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="py">set_reboot_arg1</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">42</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>set_reboot_arg1</code> property sets the value for the next boot. It does not change the current value as seen by the config parser.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Setting <code>boot_arg1</code> to 42 by using <code>vcmailbox`</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="go">sudo vcmailbox 0x0003808c 8 8 1 42</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Reading <code>boot_arg1</code> by using <code>vcmailbox`</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="go">sudo vcmailbox 0x0003008c 8 8 1 0
</span><span class="gp">#</span><span class="w"> </span>Example output – boot_arg1 is 42
<span class="gp">#</span><span class="w"> </span>0x00000020 0x80000000 0x0003008c 0x00000008 0x80000008 0x00000001 0x0000002a 0x0000000</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The value of the <code>boot_arg1</code> variable when the OS was started can be read from <a href="configuration.html#part4">device-tree</a> at <code>/proc/device-tree/chosen/bootloader/arg1</code></p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="cust_otpn">
<a class="anchor" href="#cust_otpn"></a><code>cust_otpN</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>cust_otpN</code> boot variable, where <code>N</code> is in the range 0–7, corresponds to the eight customer OTP rows.
This enables you to select different configurations based on persistent values stored in customer OTP.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This example <code>config.txt</code> snippet changes the <code>cmdline</code> file only when bit 0 of the customer OTP row 0 is set:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="nn">[cust_otp0&amp;0x1]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="py">cmdline</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">cmdline_product1.txt</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>For more information about programming customer OTP rows, see <a href="raspberry-pi.html#write-and-read-customer-otp-values">Write and read customer OTP values</a>.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="bootvar0">
<a class="anchor" href="#bootvar0"></a><code>bootvar0</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi 4 and newer devices only.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>bootvar0</code> variable is a 32-bit user-defined value that is set through <code>rpi-eeprom-config</code>, and then can be used as a conditional variable in <code>config.txt</code>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>For example, setting <code>bootvar0</code> to 42 in <code>rpi-eeprom-config</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="py">BOOTVAR0</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">42</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Using <code>bootvar0</code> conditionally in <code>config.txt</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="nn">[bootvar0=42]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="py">arm_freq</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">1000</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This allows a common image (that is, with the same <code>config.txt</code> file) to support different configurations based on the persistent <code>rpi-eeprom-config</code> settings.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="boot_count">
<a class="anchor" href="#boot_count"></a><code>boot_count</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi 5 and newer devices only.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>boot_count</code> variable is an 8-bit value stored in a reset-safe register that is incremented at boot (wrapping back to zero at 256). It is cleared if power is disconnected.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To read <code>boot_count</code> by using <code>vcmailbox</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="go">sudo vcmailbox 0x0003008d 4 4 0
</span><span class="gp">#</span><span class="w"> </span>Example - boot count is 3
<span class="gp">#</span><span class="w"> </span>0x0000001c 0x80000000 0x0003008d 0x00000004 0x80000004 0x00000003 0x00000000</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Setting/clearing <code>boot_count</code> by using <code>vcmailbox</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">#</span><span class="w"> </span>Clear boot_count by setting it to zero.
<span class="go">sudo vcmailbox 0x0003808d 4 4 0</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The value of <code>boot_count</code> when the OS was started can be read from <a href="configuration.html#part4">device-tree</a> at <code>/proc/device-tree/chosen/bootloader/count</code></p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="boot_partition-2">
<a class="anchor" href="#boot_partition-2"></a><code>boot_partition</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>boot_partition</code> variable can be used to select alternate OS files (for example, <code>cmdline.txt</code>) to be loaded, depending on which partition <code>config.txt</code> was loaded from after processing <a href="#autoboot-txt">autoboot.txt</a>. This is intended for use with an <code>A/B</code> boot-system with <code>autoboot.txt</code> where it is desirable to be able to have identical files installed to the boot partition for both the <code>A</code> and <code>B</code> images.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The value of the <code>boot_partition</code> can be different to the requested <code>partition</code> variable if it was overridden by setting <code>boot_partition</code> in <a href="#autoboot-txt">autoboot.txt</a> or if the specified partition was not bootable and <a href="raspberry-pi.html#PARTITION_WALK">PARTITION_WALK</a> was enabled in the EEPROM config.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Example <code>config.txt</code> – select the matching root filesystem for the <code>A/B</code> boot file-system:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="c"># Use different cmdline files to point to different root filesystems based on which partition the system booted from.
</span><span class="nn">[boot_partition=1]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="py">cmdline</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">cmdline_rootfs_a.txt  # Points to root filesystem A</span>
<span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[boot_partition=2]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="py">cmdline</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">cmdline_rootfs_b.txt  # Points to root filesystem B</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The value of <code>boot_partition</code> – the partition used to boot the OS – can be read from <a href="configuration.html#part4">device-tree</a> at <code>/proc/device-tree/chosen/bootloader/partition</code></p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="partition">
<a class="anchor" href="#partition"></a><code>partition</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The <code>partition</code> variable can be used to select alternate boot flows according to the requested partition number (<code>sudo reboot N</code>) or through direct usage of the <code>PM_RSTS</code> watchdog register.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-tryboot-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-tryboot-filter"></a>The <code>[tryboot]</code> filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This filter succeeds if the <code>tryboot</code> reboot flag was set.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>It is intended for use in <a href="#autoboot-txt">autoboot.txt</a> to select a different <code>boot_partition</code> in <code>tryboot</code> mode for fail-safe OS updates.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The value of <code>tryboot</code> at the start of boot can be read from <a href="configuration.html#part4">device-tree</a> at <code>/proc/device-tree/chosen/bootloader/tryboot</code></p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-edid-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-edid-filter"></a>The <code>[EDID=*]</code> filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>When switching between multiple monitors while using a single SD card in your Raspberry Pi, and where a blank config isn’t sufficient to automatically select the desired resolution for each one, this allows specific settings to be chosen based on the monitors' EDID names.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To view the EDID name of an attached monitor, you need to follow a few steps. Run the following command to see which output devices you have on your Raspberry Pi:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">ls</span> <span class="nt">-1</span> /sys/class/drm/card?-HDMI-A-?/edid</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>On a Raspberry Pi 4, this will print something like:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre>/sys/class/drm/card1-HDMI-A-1/edid
/sys/class/drm/card1-HDMI-A-2/edid</pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You then need to run <code>edid-decode</code> against each of these filenames, for example:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>edid-decode /sys/class/drm/card1-HDMI-A-1/edid</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If there’s no monitor connected to that particular output device, it’ll tell you the EDID was empty; otherwise it will serve you <strong>lots</strong> of information about your monitor’s capabilities. You need to look for the lines specifying the <code>Manufacturer</code> and the <code>Display Product Name</code>. The "EDID name" is then <code>&lt;Manufacturer&gt;-&lt;Display Product Name&gt;</code>, with any spaces in either string replaced by underscores. For example, if your <code>edid-decode</code> output included:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre>....
  Vendor &amp; Product Identification:
    Manufacturer: DEL
....
    Display Product Name: 'DELL U2422H'
....</pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The EDID name for this monitor would be <code>DEL-DELL_U2422H</code>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can then use this as a conditional-filter to specify settings that only apply when this particular monitor is connected:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="nn">[EDID=DEL-DELL_U2422H]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="py">cmdline</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">cmdline_U2422H.txt</span>
<span class="nn">[all]</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>These settings apply only at boot. The monitor must be connected at boot time, and the Raspberry Pi must be able to read its EDID information to find the correct name. Hotplugging a different monitor into the Raspberry Pi after boot will not select different settings.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>On the Raspberry Pi 4, if both HDMI ports are in use, then the EDID filter will be checked against both of them, and configuration from all matching conditional filters will be applied.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
This setting is not available on Raspberry Pi 5.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-serial-number-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-serial-number-filter"></a>The serial number filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Sometimes settings should only be applied to a single specific Raspberry Pi, even if you swap the SD card to a different one. Examples include licence keys and overclocking settings (although the licence keys already support SD card swapping in a different way). You can also use this to select different display settings, even if the EDID identification above is not possible, provided that you don’t swap monitors between your Raspberry Pi devices. For example, if your monitor doesn’t supply a usable EDID name, or if you are using composite output (from which EDID cannot be read).</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To view the serial number of your Raspberry Pi, run the following command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">cat</span> /proc/cpuinfo</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>A 16-digit hex value is displayed near the bottom of the output. The serial number of your Raspberry Pi is the last eight hex-digits. For example, if you see:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
