<pre>Serial          : 0000000012345678</pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The serial number is <code>12345678</code>.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
On some Raspberry Pi models, the first 8 hex-digits contain values other than <code>0</code>. Even in this case, only use the last eight hex-digits as the serial number.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can define settings that will only be applied to this specific Raspberry Pi:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="nn">[0x12345678]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># settings here apply only to the Raspberry Pi with this serial
</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[all]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># settings here apply to all hardware
</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="the-gpio-filter">
<a class="anchor" href="#the-gpio-filter"></a>The GPIO filter</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can also filter depending on the state of a GPIO. For example:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="nn">[gpio4=1]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># Settings here apply if GPIO 4 is high
</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[gpio2=0]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># Settings here apply if GPIO 2 is low
</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[all]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># settings here apply to all hardware
</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="combine-conditional-filters">
<a class="anchor" href="#combine-conditional-filters"></a>Combine conditional filters</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Filters of the same type replace each other, so <code>[pi2]</code> overrides <code>[pi1]</code>, because it is not possible for both to be true at once.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Filters of different types can be combined by listing them one after the other, for example:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="c"># settings here apply to all hardware
</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[EDID=VSC-TD2220]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># settings here apply only if monitor VSC-TD2220 is connected
</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[pi2]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># settings here apply only if monitor VSC-TD2220 is connected *and* on a Raspberry Pi 2
</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="nn">[all]</span><span class="w">
</span><span class="c"># settings here apply to all hardware
</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Use the <code>[all]</code> filter to reset all previous filters and avoid unintentionally combining different filter types.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="memory-options">
<a class="anchor" href="#memory-options"></a>Memory options</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/config_txt/memory.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="total_mem">
<a class="anchor" href="#total_mem"></a><code>total_mem</code>
</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This parameter can be used to force a Raspberry Pi to limit its memory capacity: specify the total amount of RAM, in megabytes, you wish the Raspberry Pi to use. For example, to make a 4 GB Raspberry Pi 4B behave as though it were a 1 GB model, use the following:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="py">total_mem</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">1024</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This value will be clamped between a minimum of 128 MB, and a maximum of the total memory installed on the board.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="licence-key-and-codec-options">
<a class="anchor" href="#licence-key-and-codec-options"></a>Licence key and codec options</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/config_txt/codeclicence.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Hardware decoding of additional codecs on the Raspberry Pi 3 and earlier models can be enabled by <a href="https://codecs.raspberrypi.com/license-keys/">purchasing a licence</a> that is locked to the CPU serial number of your Raspberry Pi.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The Raspberry Pi 4 has permanently disabled hardware decoders for MPEG2 and VC1. These codecs cannot be enabled, so a hardware codec licence key is not needed. Software decoding of MPEG2 and VC1 files performs well enough for typical use cases.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The Raspberry Pi 5 has H.265 (HEVC) hardware decoding. This decoding is enabled by default, so a hardware codec licence key is not needed.</p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="decode_mpg2">
<a class="anchor" href="#decode_mpg2"></a><code>decode_MPG2</code>
</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>decode_MPG2</code> is a licence key to allow hardware MPEG-2 decoding, for example, <code>decode_MPG2=0x12345678</code>.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="decode_wvc1">
<a class="anchor" href="#decode_wvc1"></a><code>decode_WVC1</code>
</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>decode_WVC1</code> is a licence key to allow hardware VC-1 decoding, for example, <code>decode_WVC1=0x12345678</code>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If you have multiple Raspberry Pi devices and you’ve bought a codec licence for each of them, you can list up to eight licence keys in a single <code>config.txt</code>, for example <code>decode_MPG2=0x12345678,0xabcdabcd,0x87654321</code>. This enables you to swap the same SD card between the different Raspberry Pi devices without having to edit <code>config.txt</code> each time.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="video-options">
<a class="anchor" href="#video-options"></a>Video options</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/config_txt/video.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="hdmi-mode">
<a class="anchor" href="#hdmi-mode"></a>HDMI mode</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To control HDMI settings, follow the instructions in <a href="configuration.html#display-settings">Display settings</a> in the <strong>Configuration</strong> page.</p>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="hdmi-pipeline-for-4-series-devices">
<a class="anchor" href="#hdmi-pipeline-for-4-series-devices"></a>HDMI Pipeline for 4-series devices</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To support dual displays and modes up to 4Kp60, Raspberry Pi 4B, Compute Module 4, Compute Module 4S, and Raspberry Pi 400 generate two output pixels per clock cycle.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Every HDMI mode has a list of timings that control all the parameters around sync pulse durations. These are typically defined through a pixel clock, and then a number of active pixels, a front porch, sync pulse, and back porch for each of the horizontal and vertical directions.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Running everything at 2 pixels per clock means that the 4-series devices cannot support a timing where <em>any</em> of the horizontal timings are not divisible by 2. The firmware and Linux kernel filter out any mode that does not fulfil this criteria.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>There is only one incompatible mode in the CEA and DMT standards: DMT mode 81, 1366x768 @ 60Hz. This mode has odd-numbered values for the horizontal sync and back porch timings and a width that indivisible by 8.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If your monitor has this resolution, 4-series devices automatically drop down to the next mode advertised by the monitor; typically 1280x720.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="hdmi-pipeline-for-5-series-devices">
<a class="anchor" href="#hdmi-pipeline-for-5-series-devices"></a>HDMI Pipeline for 5-series devices</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi 5 and later, Raspberry Pi Compute Module 5 and later, and Raspberry Pi 500 and later, also work at two output pixels per clock cycle. These models have special handling for odd timings and can handle these modes directly.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="composite-video-mode">
<a class="anchor" href="#composite-video-mode"></a>Composite video mode</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Composite video output can be found on each model of Raspberry Pi computer:</p>
</div>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><thead><tr><th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">model</th>
<th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">composite output</th>
</tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Raspberry Pi 1 A and B</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">RCA jack</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Raspberry Pi Zero</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Unpopulated <code>TV</code> header</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Raspberry Pi Zero 2 W</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Test pads on underside of board</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Raspberry Pi 5</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">J7 pad next to HDMI socket</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">All other models</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">3.5 mm AV jack</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
Composite video output is not available on Keyboard models.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="enable_tvout">
<a class="anchor" href="#enable_tvout"></a><code>enable_tvout</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Set to <code>1</code> to enable composite video output and <code>0</code> to disable. On Flagship models since Raspberry Pi 4, Compute Modules since CM4, and Zero models, composite output is only available if you set this to <code>1</code>, which also disables HDMI output. Composite output is not available on Keyboard models.</p>
</div>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><thead><tr><th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Model</th>
<th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Default</th>
</tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Flagship models since Raspberry Pi 4B, Compute Modules since CM4, Keyboard models</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">0</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">All other models</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">1</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="paragraph">
<p>On supported models, you must disable HDMI output to enable composite output. HDMI output is disabled when no HDMI display is detected. Set <code>enable_tvout=0</code> to prevent composite being enabled when HDMI is disabled.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To enable composite output, append <code>,composite</code> to the end of the <code>dtoverlay=vc4-kms-v3d</code> line in <a href="../computers/config_txt.html#what-is-config-txt"><code>/boot/firmware/config.txt</code></a>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="py">dtoverlay</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">vc4-kms-v3d,composite</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>By default, this outputs composite NTSC video. To choose a different mode, instead append the following to the single line in <code>/boot/firmware/cmdline.txt</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="ini"><span class="py">vc4.tv_norm</span><span class="p">=</span><span class="s">&lt;video_mode&gt;</span></code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Replace the <code>&lt;video_mode&gt;</code> placeholder with one of the following values:</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p><code>NTSC</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>NTSC-J</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>NTSC-443</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>PAL</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>PAL-M</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>PAL-N</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>PAL60</code></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>SECAM</code></p>
</li>
</ul></div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="lcd-displays-and-touchscreens">
<a class="anchor" href="#lcd-displays-and-touchscreens"></a>LCD displays and touchscreens</h3>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="ignore_lcd">
<a class="anchor" href="#ignore_lcd"></a><code>ignore_lcd</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>By default, the Raspberry Pi Touch Displays are used when detected on the I2C bus. <code>ignore_lcd=1</code> skips this detection phase. This prevents the LCD display from being used.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="disable_touchscreen">
<a class="anchor" href="#disable_touchscreen"></a><code>disable_touchscreen</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Enables and disables the touchscreen.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>disable_touchscreen=1</code> disables the touchscreen component of the official Raspberry Pi Touch Displays.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="generic-display-options">
<a class="anchor" href="#generic-display-options"></a>Generic display options</h3>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="disable_fw_kms_setup">
<a class="anchor" href="#disable_fw_kms_setup"></a><code>disable_fw_kms_setup</code>
</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>By default, the firmware parses the EDID of any HDMI attached display, picks an appropriate video mode, then passes the resolution and frame rate of the mode (and overscan parameters) to the Linux kernel by using settings on the kernel command line. In rare circumstances, the firmware can choose a mode not in the EDID that might be incompatible with the device. Use <code>disable_fw_kms_setup=1</code> to disable passing video mode parameters, which can avoid this problem. The Linux video mode system (KMS) instead parses the EDID itself and picks an appropriate mode.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
On Raspberry Pi 5, this parameter defaults to <code>1</code>.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="camera-settings">
<a class="anchor" href="#camera-settings"></a>Camera settings</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/config_txt/camera.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="disable_camera_led">
<a class="anchor" href="#disable_camera_led"></a><code>disable_camera_led</code>
</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Setting <code>disable_camera_led</code> to <code>1</code> prevents the red camera LED from turning on when recording video or taking a still picture. This is useful for preventing reflections, for example when the camera is facing a window.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="awb_auto_is_greyworld">
<a class="anchor" href="#awb_auto_is_greyworld"></a><code>awb_auto_is_greyworld</code>
</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Setting <code>awb_auto_is_greyworld</code> to <code>1</code> allows libraries or applications that do not support the greyworld option internally to capture valid images and videos with NoIR cameras. It switches auto awb mode to use the greyworld algorithm. This should only be needed for NoIR cameras, or when the High Quality camera has had its <a href="../accessories/camera.html#filter-removal">IR filter removed</a>.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
